CONFIDENTIAL
1. Authorization to discontinue mail and cable summaries
of economic material from monitored Jap broadcasts (Depart*
ment's cables of July 24 and August 21, 1942 nos. 666 and 752)
1s requested because: (a) IDC now forwards in microfilm the
information of the Ministry's daily release in Chinese; (b)
it is understood that FCC duplicates broadcasts in English
monitored locally by OWI. (Copies of OWI releases of Domei
news in English will be sent weekly via pouch if OEw desires);
(c) limitation on use of secret code.
2. Since the material has already been translated and
compiled, a despatch covering July broadcasts is being sub-
mitted by the Embassy.
ATCHESON

TOKYO'S REGULAR COMMENTATORS AND COMMENTARIES
(In mglish, ehiefly in Ameriean trans-
missions, but alse in those to Australia
and to Earope.)
1. Takee (er Tateme) Akiyam gives a series of talks entitled "The Code
of Japamese Warriers." The talke began early in July and appear every
Friday.
Akiyame gives his comments with se bedk an acsent that evem with good
reseptiem tramseription is very diffioult. His practice is to take
up poist after peint ef the Imperial Code - valor, benevolenee, unity
ete. - giving a schoalmsterly exposition of the principle and illus-
trating it with an apprepriate story from Japamás wars. In brief re-
feremees to current affairs, he seeks to show that the warrior code applies
to the matien as a whole.
The talks sound like translatione from an indoctrination course for Japan-
ese middle-schoel students, and their listener appeal may be rated accord-
ingly. Tokyo calls them "highly revealing talks."
2. alime Amme is the most frequent speaker in the "Light from the East"
commentary series, a daily feature which is one of Tokyo's oldest stand-
bys.
Asuma, who often delivers his own talks, is deseribed as "an outstanding
autherity on ourrent affairs." His voice is somewhat harsh and strident
and his delivery monotonous. His acoent is not marked.
Amuma is evidently given a wide latitude in choice of subjeet-matter, for
his diseussions range from semal immorality in the United States to
Japan's "Eakko Iahin" (universal brotherbood: Japanese style) principle.
Dessdest demeerasy is probably his faverite theme. Into it he likes to
weave the line that "Japan is fighting for what the American people were
told they entered the war, and the United States is fighting against these
ss."
Hs wse of Eaglish is smooth and surrent, and he would rate above the
average of Tokye's commentaters.
3. Jagee Dade beldly started out in June with a series oalled "The Truth
Bekind the Hows," whieh simee has besome a Tuesday feature called
"mdreek News and Previewe," plus the "Trutk" en Fridays.
Intredesing his first talk Jane 12, Tekye prondaed "...another great
Among the beokn he hes writtam are twe latest omes, tTan Iears af Reese-
veltis Adsinistratien,' and Deelime of Aagle-ameriean Prestige,: which

For five years, from 1933 to 1938, he was the New York correupondent of
the Tokye Nichi Nichi and the Osake Mainichi. On his return to Japan
be became one of the most (..word..) writers of magazine articles on
world affairs, especially America."
Like Amume, he turns his attention in many directions -- Indian inde-
pendemoe, Tojo's utterances, American domestic confusion, and the
construction of the oo-prosperity sphere. Also like Amum, he often
appears personally; and his style is eesy and colloquial.
4. Ken Gotg, "the well-kmown commentator on current internaticnal
affairs," comes on Thursdays two or three times a month. The evi-
dence at hand indicates that he speaks only in the transmission to
Australia and the South Seas, which, incidentally, is also intended
to hit the Allied troops.
Therefore it is noteworthy that some of his com entaries have sought
to derogate American Facific stretegy. A recent cne professed to
"sympathize with the weary American scldiers on the South Pacifio
1slands" who have to go through the "ordeal" of a " jungle by jungle"
campaign against the specially trained Japanese. Otherwise his

5. Shigee Hata was introduced May 1l in a "series of commentaries which
bage been written with the purpose in mind of starting a train of
thought in listeners' minds leading to a better understanding and
awareness of what the world is going through today." He opened with
a strong attempt to sell the co-prosperity sphere as "the first
conarete aotual step" toward a cooperating, peaceful world; his
first two commentaries sounded arost like peace feelers.
While Hata is still tryine tc enlizhten the uninformed American public
on the subjeot of the co-prosperity sphere, he is concentrating equally
on the ignorance of the average American about United States losses,
lack ofincere" war aims, and other such standard subjects.
The staff announcer always reads Hata's commentaries. None of the
recent ones could be termed outstanding.
6. Erangea Hopking speaks Thursdays on the "Light from the East"
program. the is the mest durable of the several foreigners Radio
Tokyo employs; en July 29 she announoed that she had been broadoasting
just a year. She is believed to be an ax-missionary, but she herself
has only said that she is "not a Cathelic."
Her voioe is high-pitehed and unpleasant, her diotion is unclear, and her
delivery is mervous and harried. The monitors coneede, howe or, that she
is improving slightly.
Franees Hopkims spesialises in deploring Americs 's retreat from humanity
and Christiaxity, holding up Japan as a model of intermatianal merality.
Ber faverite words are "Christianity," "honor," and "dishonor." A typical

His desigmation as a military commentator does not prevent him from indulging

mighty continent as she progresses in her victory march week by week."
13. The "Chgildrem's Bour" is heard Mondays and Thursdays. Most of the

And see if you oan be really good boys and girls until we meet again..."
Oocasionally the period is used as a vehiole for more positive stuff, as

I4. "The Church ms Hour", on Wednesdays, consists of a few minntes:

Very little overt propaganda is attached. The effort is apparently to

16. "Japan's Science and Medicine" has been given Sundays since June 27, when
it was inaugurated with the hope to make a contribution to the social wel-
fare of manking." It is one of the "Light from the East" presentations.
As the title implies, the series seeks to prove that Japan is well-off in
food and medical supplies, up-to-date in diet and sound of health. There
are also many references to the benefits Japan is bringing to the Asiatic
countries along these lines.

Wellowing are names of persons formerly broadcasting from Tokyo and
apled China. Some of them have not been heard recently. The back-
Found material is furnished by FBIS editors and translators on the Coast,
Trom their omn personal knowledge. It is unofficial and its accuracy
cannot be vouehed for in the absence of up-to-date information.
1. Ghagles Hiese Yoshii: He wmas heard from Tokyo until shortly
after Pearl arber, but bas been eitber silent or working under another
name sinee them. Be greduated from the University of Oregon at Bugene
in 1932. Origimally registered in class of '31 but took an extra year.
He participated little in student activities. He went to Japan in 1935
where he got a job with the Japnn Broadoastins Corporation at about 120
yem per month. The job probably came to him through Professor Takiso
Matsumoto of Meiji University who was responsible  securing Yoshii
amother post as a teacher at Keio Preparatory University. His acquaintance-
ship with ex- Poreigm Minister Matsuoka was trivial--only acquainted, in
that both were greduates of University of Oregon.
Teshii broadeast on the West Coast beam of Radio Tokyo for four years
but has now branehed out. He married the daughter of a wealthy Sendai
family and by l94l be had two children. he must be about 33 years of age.
2. Sheishi Murate, broadoasting irregularly. He is a Hawaiian who
attended the University of Hawaii for two years, then left for Japan in
1933 where be entered Waseda University and stayed six years. He used
to announee the graveyard shift for Radio Tokyo in its transmission beamed
to Barope, the South Seas and South America. He joined the Japanese Broad-

Hongkon, July 27.- The communications bureau
of the Honpkong go vernment general announced that postal
service between Honpkong and Mengohiang will be opened

as between Hongkong and Janan. The coumunications bureau
also is pushine plans to open a nostal service between

PHILIPPINE ISLANDS
93. Increased Production of Text: les in Philinpines.
anila, July 3.- Self-sufficiency in textile
articles in the Thilippines is speedily being attained
with plen after plan heing put into effect for the
acceleration in stren thening the country's cotton
in ustry. It is recalled that most of the textiles
was imported from Taran prior to the war. Native
production averared only one percent of the local
coneum-tion...However, following the Janan se army's
arrival, a plan was irrediately established for the
increesed production of cotton ani raximum native
production of textiles. Textile vorks in the Philippines
include the Naticnal Indis trial Comnany (20.C00 snindles)
and the Philippine Cotton Mill (, Co srirdles). The
present 20,00 snindles are busilv engaged for long hours
in sninning out high grade textile articles. Articles
manufactured presently are being supplied to those engared
in reconstructin develonment projects. Since the reserve
stock of articles imported prior to the war was abundant,
free purchasine was permitted even prior to the ar ival
of the Ja anese army until April this year. However,
from ay the coupon system of sales will be instituted
for a more rational distribution of textiles. The military
administration has decided to increase the textile pro-
duction facil:ties to three times the present size. Stens
are already being prepared for irporting spinning and
weaving faoilities from Janan, of which 50,000 srindles
will be allocated in the Thilippines. The question of
raw materials is expected to be solved with the successful
operation of the second and third year plen of production,
now that the first year has been completed with great
success. The National Industrial Company is now experi-
mentine with encouragine results in the production of
textiles with 40 percent hemp mixed with cot ton. It is
expeoted that this will speed up the solution of the
raw cotton question. Lately also success has been
achieved in manufacturine oloth in which wool consists
of cotton warp of hemp (?). N'eanwhile, this year's
harvest of raw cotton is rapidly being converted into
teable tertiles by the National Industrial Company.

JAPANESE MPIRE
1. Aluminum Production in First Quarter Exceeded
Goal.
Tokyo, July 2.- Bolstering immeasurably Japan's
warplane production for the prosecution of the current
war whioh is entering upon its decisive stage, Japan's
aluminum industry achieved remarkable results in the
first quarter period of this year with the production
far exceeding the goal, the Yomiuri-Hochi reported.
Prompted by the urgent necessity to Inorease Japan's air
force to execute the war to its victorous end, the
Govermment adopted appropriate measures to stimulate
increased aluminum production. Parallel with govermmental
efforts, the civilian light metals control body extended
all possible aid to the industry. The journal specially
pointed out the remarkable improvement in quality attained
by the aluminum industry. More than 95 peroent of this
year's production has a metal value of more than 99.3
pereent in contrast with last year's product which was
91 pereent... (Domei in English).
2. Production of Five Koy Industries in Korea
Boing Pushed.
Tokyo, July 2.- Plans to inorease the production
of the five key industries in Chosen, particularly iron
ore and light metals, are being pushed by the Chosen
Government General, the Nippon Sangyo revealed today.
Regarding the increase of iron ore production, measures
facilitating mining and effeetive utilisation of ore
dust as well as the enlargement of steel manufacturing
by the electric furnace method have been considered.
The Govermment General is also encouraging maximum
production of light metals by expansion of aluminum and
magnesium factories and the construction of new magnesium
plants as well as speeding the completion of installations
of the Sumitomo Aluminum Works and the Showa Eleetrio
Works. (Dome1 in English).
3. Invention of Lubrioatims Oil Renovator Reported.
Tokyo, July 2.- In view of the inoreasing demand
for lubrieating oils, the Japen Eleetric Maehinery
Industries, Tokyo, has imvented a lubrieating oil
renovator.

=== Page 36 === (confidence 0.99)
-2-
renovator. The plan is to collect used lubricants for
refining by the renovator; excellent lubricating oils
can be produced. These renovators will be manufactured
in large quantities soon. Steam-propelled vessels from
20 to 250 tons may use such a renovator. The lubricating
oil refined by the renovator contains only one to two
pereent of water and is therefore the same as new lubricat-
ing oil (Domei in Japanese).
4. Training Youths to be Agricultural Exports
In South.
Tokyo, July 2.- The Agriculture and Forestry
Ministry will open the fourth training season shortly
at Yatsugatake, Nagano prefecture, for 200 selected
youths to be sent to the southern rogions. Aiming at
the introduction of advanced Japanese agricultural
methods and to improve agricultural yield in the southern
rogions, a four months' course of intensive training is
given to the youths between the ages of 18 and 25, destined
to be worthy leaders of the southern regions. (Domei in
English).
Tokyo, July 2.- Farm management and cultivation
methods of the natives in the occupied areas in the southern
regions are all vory backward. It has now been proved that
the production can be increased three or four fold by
adoption of Japanese methods of farming. The Ministry of
Agriculture and Forestry has therefore decided to train
a number of directors for the improvement of agriculture
there. The central training farm at Nagano will give
training to such personnel to be despatohed to the
southorn regions. Graduates from agrioultural schools
or similar institutions, aged 18 to 25, of good character,
health and thoughts, may enroll. Two hundred students
will be admitted for training. The instructors will be
selected from among the staff of the Ministry of Agri-
culture and Forestry and other agricultural authorities.
(Domei in Japanese).
5. New Silicon Mines Discovered in Ibaraki Prefeoture.
Tokyo, July 3.- There has been an increasing
demand for silicon which is indispensable for grinding
and moulding in the light metal industries for strengthen-
ing the fighting potential. Recently, a silicon deposit
of excellent quality ha been discovered in the pine
forest region in front of the railway station in South
Central village, Taga town, Ibaraki prefecture. This
deposit contains as much as 85 percent of silica and is
the ideal ore for making moulds. Moreover, the proximity
of the mines to the station, only l0 minutes distant by
walking, make transportation very convedient. The deposit
is only three feet below the surface of the ground. (Domei
in Japanese).
6. Substitute Soya Bean Sauce from Copra Cake.
Tokyo, July 8.- A new method of brewing "shoyu"
or soya bean sauce from copra cake instead of from
soybeans

=== Page 37 === (confidence 0.99)
-3-
soybeans and wheat was completed by Eiji Oyama, chemist
at the army eentral provisions department. Finding that
copre eake contaimed similar ingredients as soybeans,
Oyama, assisted by two Noda Shoya Manufacturing Company
experts, erperimented for two months before suecessfully
produeing synthetic shoyu. Already nearly 2,000,000
gallons of the liquid have been produced and army
authorities plan to carry out large soale production
which will not only meet army but also civilian needs.
It is understood that the use of copra bagasse will en-
able the diversion of 200,000 tons of soybeans and
133,000 tons of wheat, presently used to manufacture
shoyu, for other purposes. Synthetic shoyu not only has
an exeellent flavor but can be kept for a longer period
without spoiling. Since special equipment is not needed
to manufacture it, the present shoyu piants can immediately
begin turning out copra cake shoyu. Another feature of
synthetic shoyu is that it is ready for the market within
two months while soybean shoyu required at least one year.
(Domei in English).
7. New Method of Dressing Iron Sand Patented.
Tokyo, July l0.- Patent bureau ofricials disclosed
that a novel invention for dressing iron sand recently
was patented. They said that the patented process to
remove technical dirriculties hitherto attended in dress-
ing iron sand, which is abundantly available in the sea-
shore of Japan, will contribute a great deal to wartime
iron production. "The dry process for dressing iron
sand" was invented by Shigeaki Fujita and Ryukiehi
Takazuka in Fukuoka. Because of the big improvement in
ordinary magnetic dressing the machine newly invented is
one highly efficient. In place of a brass revolving
disc an iron one is used and the magnetic force has been
intensified about 20 times and furthermore centrifugal
force is utilized. Dried sand containing iron sand drops
on the disc revolving at high speed and all non-magnetic
matters scatter and fall from the disc while such
magnetic matter as iron sand remains on the disc because
of the electromagnet. (Domei in English).
8. Treatment of Wheat and Barley Seeds with Roentgen
Rays.
Tokyo, July 13.- Prof. Hitoshi Kihara, department
of agriculture of the Kyoto Imperial University, recent
academy medalist, succeeded in producing a specie...
(garble)...with cultivation period shorter by one month
than ordinary species. Prof. Kihara's new precess
requires the exposure of wheat seeds to high voltage
Roentgen rays for two hours. Differing from other
methods employing high frequeney electrie current,
Kihara's methods will permanently change the hereditary
charecter of seeds of wheat through a single applieation
of the rays. Pref. Kihare is also experimenting with
barley, which when suceessful, will result in the in-
creased preduetion of feodstuffs in general as well as
mimimiging seld weather damage to crops in northeast
Japam. (Domei in Emglish).

9. Reports of Fourth Plenary Session of Central
Cooperative Council of Taisei Yokusanka1
(Imperial Rule Assistance Association) at
Tokyo, Ju1y 14-16, 1943.
Remarks of General Suzuki, President of
Planning Board.
Tokyo, July 14.- Addressinp the first day session
of the general meeting of the central cooperative council
of Taisei Yokusankai, Lieut. Gen. Teiichi Suzuki, presi-
dent of the Planning Board, stressed the necessity of
completing the projected readjustment of the industrial
set up to bolster the fighting power of the nation to
the highest efriciency. Stating that in the last half
of 1942 and in the first querter of this year wa pro-
duction attained unparallel results, Suzuki declared it
is necessary to further increase the fighting power of the
nation by utilizing the strength of the entire people.
Although direct expansion of the fighting power of the
nation lies in increased production of airplanes,
ships and other arms, Suzuki pointed out that the basis
of increased production of these arms lie in the general
industrial fiolds. He pointed out that the present read-
justment of industrial enterprises aims at focussing all
elements on production in crder to enhance fighting power.
He added for that this purpose it 1s neceseary to pass
a law enabling...supply labor for war industries. (Domei
in English).
Tokyo, July 14.- On July 14 in the fourth plenary
session of the central cooperative council of Taisel
Yokusankai, Lieut. Gen. Suzuki, chief of the Planning
Board, delivered a speech on the special features and
enforcement of the industrial readjustments law, passed
by the last session of the Diet for the furtherance of
fighting strength. He asked the officials and the people
to cooperate wholeheartedly in order to earry out the
adjustments satisfactorily and with resolution and speed.
The gist of hie speech is as follows:
Production is an important factor of fighting
strength. Satisfactory progress has been made since the
latter half of 1942. Unprecedentedly excellent results
were attained during the firat quarter of l943, during
which period the results of important industrial pro-
duotion were highly satisfactory. In order to tide over
the pressing situation of the present stage, it must be
remembered that battles can be fought successfully only
with powerful military strength. It is true that the
most urgent requirements call for the increase of air-
planes, ships and fighting ferees. It must be also
remembered, however, that the foundation thereof is very
extensive. The past industrial adjustments, due to dirri-
culties arising from eontrelled economy, were often
modified to adjustments ef a voluntary, defensive and
negative character. The imdustrial readjustments this
time aim at turning all imdustrial fecilities into fight-
ing strength. As to the plans ef adjustments, no dis-
crimimation is made as regards exeellent or unsatisfactory
businoss

=== Page 39 === (confidence 0.98)
-5-
businoss performances in the past. Industries which
can easily be turned to war production will be changed.
This is the important feature of the present industrial
adjustments. As to methods of enforoement, sufficient
labor and steel are being diverted to the increase of war
production. The effects of these industrial adjustments
on the economic order are therefore also great. Several
billion yen will be disbursed to take care of these
adjustments. In dealing with the situation, the Govern-
ment is enforoing various plans and is seeking for satis-
factory methods in order to maintain economic order and to
strengthen economic power. Now, we must thoroughly under-
stand the demands of the war on us as well as the appear-
ance of the war. We must rely on our traditional spirit
of fearing no difficulties in order to seek widespread
wartime living. (Domei in Japanese).
Tojo Admits Insufficiency of Food in Japan.
Tokyo, July 15.- Premier Tojo today attended the
fourth plenary sossion of the central cooperative council
of Taise1 Yokusankai. From 9 a.m. to 1l.30 a.m. he listened
enthusiastioally to the discussions regarding the establish-
mont of a new life for decisive war and the distribution
of daily necessities. Before he left, he said that the
wartime life of the Japanese people must be thorough.
He said that the insufficiency of food in Japan is the
result of luxury (luxurious habits?), as Japan's food
supply is more abundant than that of any other country.
The people, he said, must therefore cultivate persever-
ance. He further said: "In order to attain the objectives
of the Greater Bast Asia War, besides ensuring the liveli-
hod of our hundred million people, the livelihood of a
billion people in the Greater East Asia Co-Prosperity
Sphere must also be ensured. As to the distribution of
food, the Government will exert further efforts to over-
come difficulties. Some one recently returned to Japan
from Europe. After passing through Germany and Russia,
he arrived in Manchuria and discovered a happy world
there. Upon arriving in Japan, he felt as ir he had
entered into paradise. During wartime, the people
should know that anything which is edible can fill the
stomach and prevent hunger". (Domei in Japanese).
Insufficiency of Food Montioned in Another Roport.
Tokyo, July 16.- The chairman of the third com-
mittee of the central cooperative council of Taisei

would be handled as muoh as possible by overland facilities,
thus allocating bottoms hitherto engaged in coastal shipping
for transportation of vital materials to Japan from the
continent and the mouth. Touohing on the Government's

Hatta disclosed that the eentral council for railway

11. Fixed Prices for Tin Reduced by 48 Percent.
Tokyo, July 17.- The Ministry of Commerce and
Industry hes decided to reduce the selline prices of tin
and has ordered the Japan Metals Supply Control Company
(the national metal control organ of Japan) to effeot
the price reductions as from today. The reduced prices
(in yen per lo0 pounds) are as follow: lst grade tin -
Yen 430; 2nd grade tin - Yen 410, and 3rd grade tin -
Yen 310. The above prices show a reduction of 48 per-
cent as compared with the previous selling prices, which
were fixed one month after the outbreak of the Greater
East Asia War on the basis of the fact that 40 percent
of Japan's tin requirements had to be secured from Penang
and other places in Malaya. This was why the price of
Yen 830 per l00 pounds of first grade tin was fixed.
Now that all the South Seas regions, which produce
67 peroent of the world's total tin production, are
completely under the domination of Japanese forces, the
reductiou of prices is only a natural result. (Domei
in Japanese).

13. Positive Efforts to Inorease Production of Fibers.
Tokyo, July 18.- The South Seas regions are in
possession or all material resources indispensable to life.
Tho

=== Page 44 === (confidence 0.99)
-10-
The fibers for making quilts and clothes are one of them.
As a result of the Greater East Asia War, plans for
inoreasing production of cotton and ramie are forging
ahead in the southern regions now under Japanese domina-
tion.
Cotton: On the basis of the Greater East Asia
cotton supply structure decided unon in April, 1942, a
five-year plan for increasing production is being pushed
in the Philippines and Burma. In addition, Java, Sumatra
and the Celebes constitute a wing for this five-year plan.
Production is also being encouraged in Borneo, Indochina
and Thailand. At the end of the five-year plan, in
addition to attaining local self-suprort and self-suffi-
ciency, these regions may supnly large quantities to
Japan. During the first year (this year) of the plan,
the work is in its experimental stage in which unfavora-
ble conditions must be overcome and the results may not
come out as expected. In the Philippines, manufacture
of cotton goods has already been started with locally
produced cotton to meet local requirements. In the
experimental stage of the plan in this year, it is neces-
sary to select seeds and improve technique. It is believed
that there will be no more difficulties next year.
Ramie and Jute: These are intended to make up
the insufriciency in cotton. In the various localities,
ramie is being mixed with cotton for weaving clothing
materials. The greatest ramie plantings are in Burma,
the Philippines, south Sumatra and the coastal rogions
of east Sumatra. Ramie has been widely planted in the...
(deleted)... part of Burma. Plantations on a large
seèle may similarly be made in the Philippines. The
tea and coffee plantation areas of south Sumatra and the
Sumatra East Coast may be utilized for planting ramie.
In Java, it is planned to use vegetable farms for this
purpose. The planting of ramie in Celebes is a new venture,
which is noteworthy in the light of increasing fighting
strength. Jute production is also being furthered in all
places in the South Seas. In Burma, the first-year plan
for increasing production is being carried out. In the
Philippines and Java a five-year plan for inoreasing
production is being established. Experts and seeds are
being sent from Formosa to Burma.
New Fiber: Manila ramie is a special or exolusive
product of the Philippines. It was discovered that this
couldbe used as a material for making silk. Recently, it
has been used for making quilts and clothes. In addition,
Manila ramie may be used for making roreign clothes. In
the northeastern part of the Philippines, jute has been
produced abundantly. The area planted shows an increase
of 200 percent as compared with last year. The future is
promising. (Domei in Japanese).

Tokyo, July 20.- In the regular Cabinet meeting on
July 20 the bill for ensuring increased production of
railway rolling stock was discussed. After Lieut. Gen.
Suzuki, chief of the Planning Board, explained the details
of the bill, it was passed. The guiding principles of
this bill, as published by the Board of Information, are
as follow: In order to ensure the planned production of
railway rolling stock, there will be uniformity between
land transport and the control over production of rolling
stock. The following measures will be taken: (1) On the
basis of the National General Mobilization Act it has
been definitely established that it will be the responsi-
bility of the Minister of Railways to increase the produc-
tion of rolling stock; (2) In order fully to manifest
the manufacturing capacity for rolling stock, there will
be mixed operation of the manufacturing plants to be
directly controlled by the Ministry of Railways; (3) The
measures relating to production of rolling stock will bo
the same as for the five basic industries, and (4) War-
time regulations pertaining to control of rolling stock
will be thoroughly enforced. (Domei in Japanese).
15. Report on Rice Crops in Japan, Korea and Formose.
Tokyo, July 20.- The plan for ensuring sufficient
food will be consummated in the next fisoal year, when
Japan will abandon its reliance on imported rice. In
the present fiscal year, the growth of rice is promising.
Up to July 19, excepting a part of special areas, the
planting of rice in lo0 percent of Japan's rice fields
had been completed. It is estimated that in Chosen, up
to July 15, the total of 92.3 percent of the rice fields
had been planted. As compared with 79 percent during the
corresponding period last year, this shows an inorease
of 13.3 percent. Should there be timely rains hereafter
the plantings will be further enlarged. According to
the most conservative estimate, on the basis of 92.3
peroent plantings, 21,000,000 piculs of rice will be
harvested in the irrigated and dry fields as compared
with 15,000,000 piculs actually harvested last year. The
one-season rice plantings in Formosa will probably yield
a harvest of 3,800,000 piouls, which will be slightly
below the figure of 3,938,000 piculs actually harvested
last year. Judging from all these reports, rice production
for the present year will be greater than in l942. In
case of favorable climatio conditions, the crops of the
coming autumn will be satisfaotory. (Domei in Japanese).
16. Discovery of New Oil Filtering Process for Motor
10410100.
Tokyo, July 21.- For the discovery of a new rilter-
ing proeess, Colonel Soichi Sugitani of the imperial army
was comferred with the degree of deetor of engimeering
by

=== Page 46 === (confidence 0.99)
-12-
by the Education Ministry. His achievement was ac-
complished during the time he was busy as instructor
of the army mechanical equipment school and it is
believed it will contribute greatly to the adaptation
of motor vehicels to battlefields peculiar to regions
where Japanese forces are now fighting. Breakdowns
generally have been ascribed to engine trouble, little
attention being paid to topographical and climatic
differences. Sugitani, after experiments, reached the
conclusion that the real reason for the trouble was
not in the engine but due to the fact that oil absorbs
dust and dirt. with this basic idea, he started work
on an air purifier and filtering process for lubricat-
ing oil. His work was tentatively completed in 1939 when
the newly discovered method in book form was published
by the army tank school. He elaborated the thesis and
it was bubmitted to the Kyoto Imperial University where
he was formerly enrolled as a military student. The
thesis was apnroved by the university and he was granted
the degree on the merits of his thesis. (Domei in English).

Mongolia.

tenfold as a result of the recent completion of the
Sangohien river dam, the greatest irrigation project
over launched by the federated autonomous government of
Mongolia. The work on the construction of the titanic
irrigation systom including the dam, drainways, roads
and other accompanying facilities, was commenced two and
a half years ago with an outlay of Yen 30,000,000. (Domei
in English).
Tatung, July 12.- The construction of the Shangkan
River dam was started in September 194l and has now been
completed. This is very beneficial for the plan to increase
production in Mengchiang. The first irrigation area of
the Shangkan River includes Shanyin, Sohsien, Yinghsien,
Hwaijen and Tatung. The appropriation for conversanoy
work 1s approximately $30,000,000. The low lands will
be cultivated as rice fields. It is certainly an engineer-
ing feat. Upon completion of the conservancy work in the
first irrigation area, the annual production of kaoliang,
corn, wheat, soy beans and potatoes will be increased by
10 times. The plan for immigration to this fertile land
has also been comploted. (Domei in Japanese).
36. Industrial Developments in Mengchiang.
Kalgan, July 27.- The fedorated autonomous
government of Mongolia attained commendable results in
executing its policy and consolidating its war time
economy as exemplified in the soundness of the govern-
ment's financial devebpment and control of industry,
increased farm crops and healthy currency situation.
On July 12 the government published a general account
supplementary budget whioh totalled Yen 10,000,000.
Instead of floating bonds, the government obtained
additional revenue to meet the spending program by
advancing tax rates and using surplus funds brought
forward from the previous fiseal year. This fact in
itself extolls the soundness of the financial policy
of the government. With the approval of the additional
budget of 1943, the total budget under the general
account reaohed Yen 93,600,000.
Meanwhile, rehabilitation work on the Lungyen
iron mine, whioh was damaged by flood toward the end of
Juno,

38. Report on North China Developmont Company.
Tokyo, July l.- Following are the essential points
of a report made by the managing director of the North
China Development Company at a regular general meeting of
that company held at Tokyo on June 30:
1. Since the outbreak of the Greater East Asia War
to date very satisfactory results have been obtained in
accelerating the development of resources in north Chine,
both above and under ground, so as to increase production
and exports to Japan. North Chine is especially rich
in coal whioh is the basic strategic material, the deposits
being about 17 million (billion?) tons. Its quality and
easy accessability for mining cannot be found elsewhere.
Produotion has therefore exeeeded the quantity projected.
2. In eastern Hopei provinee and in Shantung province
there is an inexhaustible deposit of alumina shale which
1

=== Page 53 === (confidence 0.99)
-19-
is the indispensable material for the production of
alumina and fire bricks. Steady development is being
made of this product. The two salt fields at Chamglu
(Hopei) and in Shantung are the largest producers of
salt in the entire Greater East Asia Co-Prosperity
Sphere. The results of the 1942-43 fiscal year greatly
exceeded the goal. The amount of salt produced has been
sufficient to rill the export quotas to Japan and to
increase the amount to be allocated to the industries
of Greater East Asia. Plans for increasing the pro-
duction of cotton, which is one of the most important
strategic materials, are also being put into execution.
It is also anticipated that much progress will be made
in the organization of the structures for the production,
distribution and marketing of this product.
3. In Lungyen, Kinlingchen and other regions there
are rich deposits of iron ore. Steps have steadily been
taken to develop these mines in order to cope with the
increased local roquirements and shipments to other
areas. Large scale iron production plans are being put
through with the establishment of the North china Iron
Company. At the same time, small scale blast furnaces
are boing installed in various places in north China.
Some of those have already begun operations. Unon the
completion of these plans, expected in the near future,
an immense supply of steel and iron can be assured.
4. The future trend of the north China economic
situation will be the expansion in production of raw
materials and the undortaking of manufacturing in the
producing areas as much as possible in order to save
transportation facilities. For the realization of the
above, various measures are beins devised.
The amount of rainfall this year has been sufficient,
thereby promising bumper crops. It is expected that the
anxiety over food supplies will be eliminated. The
commodity price situation has been gradually improved
owing to adequate measures adopted by the authorities
concerned. In short, north China is steadily carrying
out its mission of serving as a depot (?) in the Greater
East Asia War. (Domei in Japanese).

40. Postal Service Throughout China Maintained.
Fukuoka, July 10.- Extraordinary things do
often happen in this world, in China particularly, for
despite hostilities fiercely raging between the National
Government (Nanking regime) army and Chungking forces,
normal postal service is still maintained all over
China as though nothing is happening, reported Matsujiro
Nakamura, vice chief of the post and telegraph office of
north China, upon his arrival from Peiping. The exohange
of mail bags carried out at certain points along the
Yellow River between the Nanking and Chungking postal
officials is an open secret, Nakamura said. War or no
war, the Chinese are Chinese and there is no sense in
interfering in the internal postal service which would go
on despite any form of obstruction. Late last year the
Chungking postal authorities were hard pressed by financial
deficits and ap"roached the Nanking postal authorities
proposing a raise in postal rates. Nakamura stated that
this was really going too far and the Nanking authorities
just ignored the Chungking proposal. He described the
whole episode as the eighth wonder of modern China and
typical of Chinese audacity. (Domei in English).
41. Prospects Said Bright for Bumper Crops in North
China.
Peiping, July 10.- Prospects are bright for
bumper crops in principal farm products in north China
this year, Wang Keh-min, chairman of the North China
Political Affairs Commission, said in a statement in
his concurrent capacity as chairman of the North China
Commodities' Prices Adjustment Committee. He said the
promise of good harvests, coupled with falling commodity
prices, is destined to go a long way toward solving the
food problem for the civilian population. Production
increase of cardinal war materials has been in full
swing in north China, Wang declared, pointing out the
increasing importance of the area as an important supply
base in the war of Greater East Asia, particularly since
China's declaration of war against Britain and the
United States. He disclosed that there are three
important problems for north China in accomplishing its
mission, namely, rational adjustment of prices, develop-
ment of industrial resouroes and proper control of
commodities including foodstuffs. He prophesied that
undaunted efforts by subsidiary organs of the Commodities'
Prices Adjustment Committee with the support of Japan
as well as private industrial bodies in China would
solve the wartime food problem of horth Chine despite
any difficulty. (Domei in English).

45. Establishment of North China Fiber Control Organ.
Peiping, July 19.- In order to contribute to
the proeooution of the war of East Asia, the North China
Fiber Control Association was established as a central
body to reorganize various existing organs engaged in the
production, control and distribution of cotton. The new
fiber control association, which will be under the
direct control of the North China Political Affairs
Council, aims at consolidating north China's position as
a cotton industrial center in East Asia by promoting...
(omission)...resources and improving the quality of cotton
and securing its smooth distribution. Under the leader-
ship of the fiber control association will be five import-
ant organs which are either reorganized old associations
or newly created ones, namely, the North China Cotton
Producing Promotion Company, North China Spinning Industry
Association, North China Fiber Company and the North China
Joint Enterprises Federation. The first organ will direct
efforts for the improvement of cotton seed and increase
of cotton produotion. The North China Spinning and
Weaving Industry Association will have strict control
of the cotton industry in order to effect rapid progress
both in technique and production. Expansion of acilities
will be made by bringing in idle machinery from :pan.
The North China riber Company will engage in buying and
distribution of specified fibrous goods while the North
China Fiber Association will regulate mainly the demand
and supply of cotton and other fibrous goods. A notable
feature of the new ootton industry structure in north
China is the introduotion of a bloc system among cotton
industrialists. Cotton producing areas will be divided
into four main districts, each of which will be sub-
divided into three bloos. The first division covers the
districts of Changte, Paoting and Linohing. The second
division comprises Shunte, Tientsin and Tehsien, while
the third ineludes Hantan, Peiping, and Changtien, and
the fourth covers Shihmen, Tangshan and Tsinan. (Domei
in English).

48. Self-Sufficiency 1n Foodstuffs in Shansi Province.
Taiyuan, July 22.- In order to attain self-
sufficiency in foodstuffs in Shansi province, the Japanese
military authorities with the full cooperation of the
provincial government are taking up all measures possible
for increasing food production. It is said that the need
to achieve self-sufficiency is due to the fact thet Shansi,
which is rich in mineral resources, is two-thirds covered
by mountains which make difricult the growing of foodstuffs
as well as bringing farm products from outside because of
the lack of transportation. Moreover, the increasing
urban population accompanied by industrial development
1s another reason why the province needs to raise enough
foodstuffs. Among the projeots considered by the authori-
ties to improve the food situation are the building of an
irrigation system and establishment of agricultural experi-
mental stations and buildings. Measures are also taken
to lighten the farmers' burdens by readjustment of the
land tax system. Furthermore, the authorities have reached
a decision to put into force measures based on planned
economy for the readjustment of the supnly and demand
relations. On January 4 last year the Japanese military
authorities introduced a low price policy and strengthened
the distribution and purchasing organ for cereals. The
salient points of the food polioy recently established
includes direot payments to farmers for cereals delivered
at the purohasing agenoy, enforcement of storing and
preservation of cereals. (Domei in English).

OCCUPIED CENTRAL AND SOUTH CHINA
51. Three-Year Plan for Reclamation in Kiangsu
Province.
Nanking, July l.- Details of a three-year plan
for the reclamation of land and the development of water-
ways in northern Kiangsu and along the eastern shore of
Lake Tai in southern Kiangsu were disclosed in a statement
issued by Chiang Tso-hsuan, vice minister of construction
and concurrontly chief of the bureau of water transporta-
tion. Referring to a plan for the construotion of a now
canal in northern Kiangsu, he stated that the program
calls for not only the construction of a new north-south
canal but also the establishment of water gates and creeks
running in all direotions from the new... and perfection
of irrigation and drainage facilities. A section through
which the canal will be built is wasteland near the sea,
forming the border of the Fankung dyke. The plan, which
involves both reclamation of land and cotton planting,
calls for an expenditure of Yuan 300,000,000 for the
construction of a canal 130 kilometers long as the first
project. Upon completion of this project, 12,000 acres
of land will be made available for cultivation. With
regard to dredging and reclamation of the eastern shore
of Lake Tai, in view of the danger of the eastern part
of the lake becoming lowland if left as it is, losing
its character of being a reservoir, it is planned to
reclaim the land bordering the eastern part of the lake
by dredging the waterway in order to assure a permanent
water supply, thus making the lake serve as a reservoir.
Chiang expressed the hope that investments will be forth-
coming from various quarters due to large expenditures
required for the development of the two projects. (Domei
in English).

Roconstruction Projects by Nanking Regime.

will be devoted to repairing the reinforcement of the

and also around Hungtsehu Lake, Kiangsu province, will
be undertaken. The reinforcement of the Yangtze embank-
ment will be launohed in the third year. The projects
involve annual expenditure of Yuan 20,000,000; (2) Upon
completion of the project the water level of Hungtsehu
Lake will be elevated, reclaiming approximately 7,000,000
acres of land. 270 kilometers of the new canal will be
constructed in northern Kiangsu province while 7,000
square kilometers of land will be converted into cotton
producing areas. The project will cost 300,0c0,000 yuan;
(3) With the estimate of 150,000,000 yuan, the reclamation

year plan, regaining 45,000 acres of land. Simultaneously,
the plan envisages dredging the waterway from Lake Taihu

waterway to free regions from annual floods which here-
tofore swept the area; (4) Another project envisages
preventing the Wei River from overflowing due to destruc-

said that large canal locks will be constructed, and (5)
A plan has been launohed already to check the drought in
north China which is partly caused by the loss of water
due to the destruction of the Yellow River embankments.
The plan, besides completely repairing the Yellow River
embankments and restoring the river to its previous course,
also envisages the construction of a network of canals
by the North China Government (Nanking regime?), which
is also undertaking the construction of seven new high-
ways.
The despatch disclosed that the contemplated highways
include (1) the Nanking-Shanghai road which has special

and Tsaowuchen in Chekiang province via Shaohsing is

regions; (3) the highway joining Nanking and Pengpu in

the Nanking-Pahsien road via Wuhu; (6) the Pukow-Wopi road
via Wukiang, and (7) the Pukow-Weiyin road. (Domei in

52. Taxation Policy of Nanking Regime Stated.
Nanking, July l.- Following are statements made
by the minister of finance on the taxation policy (of the
Nanking regime):
1. Customs Duties. Since the outbreak of the
Greater East Asie War revenues from import and export
duties have suffered a material setback. It is therefore
decided to make up the deficit through an increase in
the rates of transit duty. This duty will be levied in
accordance with the value of the goods instead of the
quantity. All articles will be subject to a duty 5
percent larger than at present. Excentions will be made
in the cases of soy beans and kaoliang for which the
duty will be reduced by 5 percent.
2. Consolidated Tax and Special Tax. With regard
to those luxuries and special articles in the group of
daily necessities, it is decided gradually to change the
system of taxation from the specific to ad valorem basis.
However, no drastic increase will be made in order to
avoid stimulating a corresponding rise in commodity
prices.
3. Salt Tax. Salt is not only a daily necessity
of the people but also a material needed by industries
and for military purposes. In recent years the revenue
from this tax has been on the increase due to increased
production of this commodity. Therefore no change will
be made in the rates of this tax.
4. Inoome Tax. The revenue from this tax shows
en inorease of lo0 peroent as compared with that collected
in 1940 when 1t was initiated. Further increases are
expected from the enforcement of population census in
various places. (Dome1 in Chinese).
53. Partial Revision or Kwangtung Customs Tariff Made.
Canton, July l.- The provincial government of
Kwangtung today announced a partial revision of the
Kwangtung customs tariff effective today. According to
the

=== Page 60 === (confidence 0.99)
-26-
the new revision, all specific duties in operation were
changed to ad valorem duties. The revised tariff
provides for the imposition or 2.5 percent ad valorem
duties on 16 commodities, which hitherto were subject
to specific duties, while 5 percent ad valorem duties
will be colleoted from other commodities of the "specific
duty" group under the old tarirf system. Upon enforce-
ment of the new tarifr the l00 percent special surtax
which was imposed by the old tarifr has been abolished.
It is understood that the present revision of the customs
tariff was made in view of the increasing discrepancy
between specific and ad valorem duties due to the recent
fluctuations of commodity prices. The 16 commodities
which are newly subjected to the 2.5 percent ad valorem
duty are as follow: peas and beans, kaoliang, Indian
corn, millet, bean oil, cotton seed oil, peanut oil,
rapeseed oil, sesame seed oil, peanuts, sesa e, horse-
beans, beancake, brown sugar, castor sugar (?), refined
sugar and sugar candy. (Domei in English).

Objectives of Japanese Mission Announced.
Shanghai, July 8.- The objectives of the present
first Japanese business men's economic mission to China
are to congratulate China on regaining a bright outlook
as a result of freedom from Anglo-American shackles of
the past l00 years through the recovery of the foreign
concessions and to exchange frank views with Chinese
officials and prominent business men in order to further
promote Sino-Japanese close cooperation in the fight
against foreign enemies. Though unprecedented in the
history of Sino-Japanese relations, it is a historical
inevitability and a natural trend in Greater East Asia.
From the nature of the present world war it is impossible
to construct a Co-Prosperity Sphere without complete
victory of war while the hanpiness of East Asiatio

Statements Made by Members of Japanese Mission.
Nanking, July 13.- The Japanese economic inspoction
mission to China arrived here at 9 a.m. on July 12 from
Shanghai by train. Mr. Ogura, head of the mission, as
well as Mr. Akashi and Mr. Nango, in response to inquiries
made by reporters accompanying them on the train, made
statements explaining their opinions pertaining to the
existing important issues of China such as currency, finance
and commodity prices. The gist of the statement (presumably
made by Ogura but possibly a joint effort of the members
of the mission*CHB) is as follows: While they were at
Shanghai during the past few days they candidly exchanged
ideas with the concerned Chinese there. The y can now
understand the actual state of affairs of China. The

plans must be adopted in certain spheres. On the besis
of the opinions expressed by the interested parties, they
w111

=== Page 62 === (confidence 0.98)
-28-
will discuss fully various questions in order to be of
help to the development of China's economy. China is a
country with a long history. Policies and measures
based on the recovery of Chinese racial characteristics
and maintenance of the Chinese way of life should there-
fore be adopted. Ever since Japan enforeed its new
policy vis-a-vis China, the Chinese economic field has
been stirred by great hopes and is now active in
directions indicated by these hopes. The realization
of plans will certainly be significant.
Akashi said in effect: China is in possession
of a colossal racial asset and labor for production.
If the racial asset could be induced to go to production,
it would be efficacious for the development of production
and maintenance of the value of currency. However, at
present the Chinese racial asset is not working in
concert with production, hence the continuation of un-
satisfactory economic conditions in China. This is
entirely different from Japan where asset and production
constitute one body both in appearance and in substance.
Thus, the attraction and utilization of the Chinese
racial asset is the most important issue of China today.
The gist of what Nango said is as follows: View-
ing contral China's position in the Greater East Asia
Co-Prosperity Sphere from a commercial standpoint, it is
a most important region. The promotion of trading between
central China and other parts of the Co-Prosperity Sphere
is the most urgent problem of today. When shipping is
limited in wartime, the various regions are liable to
develop local economy which is undesirable for the
development of the sphere. Thoughts must be focussed
on the Co-Prosperity Sphere as a whole. Under the total
plan, brisk trading of materials and resources is the
right procedure. for example, the cotton of central
China may be shipped to Japan; Japan's cotton cloth may
be shipped to Indochina, and Indochina's rice may be
shipped to central China. There should be logical trading
of daily necessities among all regions.
Kodera said in effect: For the prosperity of
China and Japan, it will be imperative for Japan positively
to assist China in increase of production. Regarding
spinning and weaving, besides positive efforts for the
increase of cotton production, the development of the
spinning and weaving industry will also be essential.
Thus, positive steps must be taken to transfer Japan's
spinning and weaving equipment to China so that the latter's
capital, labor, material and equipment may be actively
utilized. (Domei in Japanese).

Mombers of Mission Interviewed by Chinese
Journalists.
Nan'ing, July 15.- Two members of the Japanese
economic inspection mission to China, Mr. Voshida,
member of the board of directors of the Mitsui Bussan

operation in the Greater East Asia War. The gist of these
discussions is as follows:
Inquiry: "As the members of your mission had
sincere discussions with Chinese financial leaders

what are your feelings and plans in regard to the actual
economic conditions of China?"
Yoshida replied: "I feel that the most important
and difficult problems in Chinese economy lie in the
currenoy problem and plans pertaining to commodity prices.
As far as materials and resourees are concerned, these,
according to the present state of affairs in China, are

cooperation between the government and the people.
Currency inflation in wartime is a naturel development.
With the concerted coordination between the government
and the people, the value of the currency can certainly
be maintained".

bility. Before the outbreak of the war China had to cope
with the influence of Britain and the United States.
Thus various difficulties existed in enforcing the policy

are now entirely removed. Viewed from the Chinese and
Japanese standpoints, no difficulty exists. We feel that
the people of all places should work in concort under one
concept in order to attain the common objective".
oshida said: "In the study of economic cooperation,

your people is cherishing unnecessary worries. Japan

60. Night Navigation Allowed on Pearl River.
Canton, July 9.- To cope with the increasing
traffic in the Pearl River, night navigation will be
oponed to shipping companies from July 10, the Japanese
naval attache's office announced yesterday. It can be
recalled that navigation was limited to 22:00 hours from
April 10 this year. The announcement said that the
navigation area will consist of the main course of the
Pearl River from the Canton-Whampoa-Humen area to the
mouth of the river. Ships allowed to navigate include
ocean liners, vessels of the regular Canton-Macao line,
tug boats belonging to the Canton River Transportation
Association, Canton Warehousing Association and others
sanctioned by the naval at'ache's office. (Domei in

63. Sino-Japanese Oil and Food Committee Being
Formed.
Shanghai, July 13.- In one of the most decisive

foodstuffs and in line with government measures effectively
to prevent the rising price tendency, the establishment

formation of this committee will round out an elaborate
lower structure of commercial control organization which
now has a most formidable apparatus...The joint Japan-
China Oil and Food Committee will be composed of prominent
Japanese and Chinese businessmen whose activities have
mainly been concerned with supplying Shanghai with essen-
tial foodstuffs. After its inauguration the committee
will take up the responsibility of controlling trade in
whet and other grains, sundry foodstuffs, seeds and other
matedals necessary for the manufacture of cooking and
other edible oils and a variety of other items concerned
with the foodstuffs industry. The present plans, it was
revealed, provide for the offices of a chairman and two
vice chairmen who will conduct the activities of the
committee under the supervision of the National Commercial
Control Association. Economic observers declared that the
ostablishment of the committee augurs well for the future
as a regular supply of edible and cooking oil which is
one of the most important foodstuff items will be availa-
ble to the Chinese population. (Domei in English).

government was contemplating a readjustment of the taxa-

gave assurance that the issuance of bonds to meet the

84. Japanese Give Up Tax Exemption Rights.
Nanking, July 31.- Javanese residents in China in
the past enjoyed the special right of not having to pay
taxes to the Chinese Government. That right is a part of
the extrality rights. However, in accordance with the
treaty of transfer of tax imposition right signed between
Japan and China on July 31, these Jananese residents,
will, effective from August 1, be obliged to observe
China's tax impositions. By virtue of this treaty,
practically the entire tax imposition right has been
transferred to the National Government of China (Nanking
regime). Such a step will of course prove beneficial for
the enhancement of finances of the government. The
transfer of the tax imposition right this time covers
wine, tobacco, cotton and silk woven goods and includes
the consolidated taxes ex godowns, salt tax, income tax
and business tax. Through this transfer of right, the
tax revenue of China will be increased by approximately
CRB100,000,000 (Yen 20,000,000). In enfor01ng the
collection of these taxes, the income tax and business
tax of Japanese residents in China will be directly
affected and will constitute a duplication of taxation.
The Japanese and Chinese authorities will make the best
possible adjustments...(Source not stated: probably Domei
in Chinese or Jananese!.

Yen 16,000, and from Malaya and Sumatra Yen 7,000.

Expansion of Industry in Hongkong-Kowloon Area.
Hongkong, July 24.- Under the aegis of the
government general various factories in Hongkong and
Kowloon are now engaged in increasing the production
of necessary commodities greatly for the satisfactory
prosecution of the Greater East Asia War. A phenomenal
increase has been witnessed especially in weaving
industries and in the production of rubber, Manila rope,
sugar, clothing and beer, as well as in cold storage goods.
Before the outbreak of the war there were more than 700
factories concerned with light industry in Hongkong and
Kowloon. Simultaneously with the occupation of the
Hongkong area by Jananese forces in December, 194l, all
these factories were placed under Jananese hands and read-
justed and merged to half the number to increase efficienoy.
Recont conditions in various light industry factories in
the Hongkong-Kowloon area reveal great inprovement in
both the quality and quantity of fabric and rubber
produets under the direct management of Hongkong. Of the
various kinds of rubber, boots and other goods connected
with medical equipment are now being successfully manu-
factured. Weaving plants are now turning out tons of
superior grade canvas every month and are aiming at increas-
ing their output still further. Presently a certain
company, which has been commissioned by the government
goneral, is engaged in the manufacture of Manila rope and
has arranged to obtain more materials for its manufacture.
Also commissioned by the government general, another company
is now engaged in managing foodstuffs factories and seeing
that they meet the requirements. The same company 1s also
turning out butter and is already exporting large quantities
of th: product to various parts of the Greater East Asia
Sphere. Cube sugar required for Japanese home consumption
is also being manufactured by Hongkong-Kowloon factories...
Bleaching power as well as caustic soda is also being
produced. of the total output of Hongkong-manufactured
produots, 70 percent is exported while the balance is
consumed locally. (Domei in English).

PHILIPPINE ISLANDS
93. Increased Production of Tertiles in Philippines.
Manila, July 3.- Self-surficiency in textile
articles in the Philippines is speedily being attained
with plan after plan being put into effect for the
acceleration in strengthening the country's cotton
industry. It is recalled that most of the textiles
was imported from Janan prior to the war. Native
production averaged only one percent of the local
consumption...However, following th Japanese army's
arrival, a plan was immediately established for the
increased production of cotton and maximum native
produotion of textiles. Textile works in the Philippines
include the National Industrial Company (20,000 spindles)
and the Philippine Cotton Mill (8,O0o spindies). The
present 20,000 spindles are busily engaged for long hours
in spinning out high grade textile articles. Articles
manufactured presently are being supplied to those engaged
in reconstruction development projects. Since the reserve
stock of articles imported prior to the war was abundant,
free purchasing was permitted even prior to the arrival
of the Jananese army until April this year. However,
from May the coupon system of sales will be instituted
for a more rational distribution of textiles. The military
administration has decided to increase the textile pro-
duction facilities to three times the present size. Steps
are already being prepared for importing spinning and
weaving faoilities from Japan, of which 50,000 spindles
will be allocated in the Philippines. The question of
raw materials is expected to be solved with the successful
operation of the seoond and third year plan of production,
now that the first year has been completed with great
success. The National Industrtal Company is now experi-
menting with encouraging results in the produotion of
textiles with 40 peroent hemp mixed with cotton. It is
expected that this will speed up the solution of the
raw cotton question. Lately also success has been
aehieved in manufaoturing cloth in which wool consists
of ootton warp of hemp (?). Meanwhile, this year's
harvest of raw cotton is rapidly being converted into
usable textiles by the National Industrial Company.
(Domei in English).

wartime increase of commodity prices. Of the total bank

in time deposits, rising by about Pesos 3,300,000. Special

30,000,000. While the sharp increase in current deposits

NETHERLANDS EAST INDIES
108. Hew Juridieal System Inaugurated in Java.
Batavia, July 1.- The new juridical system in
Java, replacing the former system imposed by the Dutch
regime under whieh diseriminatory treatment was given
the

=== Page 82 === (confidence 0.99)
-48-
the native inhabitants as well as other Asiatic peoples,
will be initiated from today, according to a decree issued
by the Japanese military administration in Java. Under
the new system all cases previously tried in the suprome
court, including civil and criminal cases in which Dutch
Government officials as well as white people were
involved, will be tried in local civil or criminal courts.
Cases hitherto provisionally placed under the jurisdiction
of courts under the Japanese military administration by
decree promulgated in April this year were also transferred
to their respective local civil or criminal courts. (Domei
in English).
109. Revised Regulations for Native Officials in Jave.
Batavia, July l.- Native officials greatly
rejoiced as regulations concerning civil appointment and
salary will become effective from September l. Since the
enforcement of the military administration in Java, the
local inhabitants acting as officials have been cooperat-
ing with Japan enthusiastically for the construction of a
new Java and have been rendering great service. In March
this year, on the occasion of the first anniversary of
the occupation of Java, the military administration made
public the civil service system regulations in order to
stabilize native officiels' living and to make them
realize that they are the leaders of the people. Detailed
regulations to be made effective on September 1 this year
in respect to native customs, manners, systems and ideal
of Greater East Asia made the bases for the new system.
Sixty million Tavanese are thankful for the announcement
of the civil service system regulations on the heels of
Premier Tojo's declaration in the Diet promising the
Indonesians permission to participate in governmental
administration. (Domei in English).
110. Census of Soerabaya to be Taken August 1.
Soerabaya, July 2.- The swift progress which
Soerabaya has made in all spheres of municipal activities
since its extrication from Dutch clutches will be recorded
when the municipal census, the first to be taken here since
the outbreak of the war in Greater East Asia, will be taken
on August l. Experts believe the population, which made
a noteworthy increase parallel with the return of prosperity
recently, nowwell tops half a million. The census will be
conducted by 600 census takers who will be appointed from
among the native Javanese after one week's training by
experts from Japan. (Dome1 in English).

of this month, thanks to efforts of the Spice Manufactur-
1ng Company, which was established on April 29 in Macassar.
The manufacturing of spices was never undertaken under the
Dutch regime despite the fact that neighboring islands
such as Ambon, Halmaheira, Banda and Sanghie have long
been known as most ideal places for raising sueh spice
plants

construction of New Guinea toward the goal of self-
sufficiency in foodstuffs and increased production of
other products are rapidly progressinp. To sink or
swim with the fighting going on, every second counts.
Simultaneously with war operations, production of war
materials is being carried out for the construction of
an invincible base against Britain and the United States.
The New Guinea naval administration, after completion of
explorations and investigations and experimental pro-
duction of rice and vegetables, have plenned a production
program designed for securing military supplies on the
spot. The production program has been started with the
mobilization of 200,000 native inhabitents.
Chief among the projects to be carried out ...
are firstly, mining of coal and manganese ore, secondly,
planting rice and vegetables on already cultivated lands,
thirdly, tunny and bonito fishing and their processing;
fourthly, lumbering and ship building, and fifthly,
tapping damar and other resins, extraction of cocount
oil and manufacture of soap. Since the native living
standard is low and the staple diet is obtained from
sago palm and other native products with a plentiful
supply of fish and vegetables, their only need, in orde
to assure food supply, is to increase rice production.
The policy being followed is to utilize local labor as
much as possible for local production as early as possi-
ble. For instance, salt is manufactured by boiling sea
water with wood as fuel. Caustic soda nroduced from
salt is mixed with cocount oil to produce excellent soap.
Production of salt and soap on a big scale is possible
due to abundance of natural gas and cocount oil.
Native carpenters will be mobilized for the
construction of wooden vessels on a large scale. Experi-
mental rice planting proved that large scale rice cuiti-
vation is possible even without the use of fertilizers.
Second crop planting has been started by an agricultural
company. Vegetables and fruits are secured from planta-
tions in nine places. The tapning of dammar resin is
now greater than in prewar days. The supply of marine
products is not only surficient to meet local requirements
but also can be utilized for canning and processing into
dried bonito. A factory is now being constructed for
this. From the beginning, a controlled distribution
system under fixed prices was enforced in order to control
prices. This system has been gradumlly extended to local
manufactures such as lumber, coconuts, cogonutroil, salt,
lighting oil and soap. There is no fear of rising priees.
Thus

=== Page 84 === (confidence 0.99)
-50-
Thus production is everything in New Guinea inasmuch as
increased production meane victory. New Guinea is rising
up from a primitive state and growing up as an advanced
production base desnite enemy bombing attacks. (Domei
in English).

Macassar, July 7.- Arrangements are now being

114. Substitute Wool Froduced from Wild Silkworm.

discovery will not only assure Java of Sufficient clothing

castor bean. The silk cocoon is ready to be unreeled
in only 15 days after the silkworm begins weaving the
cocoon. (Dome1 in English).

unknown to the inhabitants of Celebes and Borneo. Even

130. Effective Drug for Amoebic Dysentery Claimed.
Kuala Lumpur, July 20.- An effective drug for
amoebic dysentery known as metine is now obtainable
in large quantities from the roots of ipecacuanha, which
is being cultivated on an extensive scale in the central
part of the l'alay Peninsula. The ipecacuanha plant,
indispensable for the manufacture of emetine, heretofore
was cultivated only at several places in the world since
it requires favorable climatic conditions. The slow
growing plant takes at least three years under good
weather conditions before emetine can be extracted so
that it was well nigh impossible to find a suitable area
for the cultivation of this important plant. The British
had been planting it on a small scale in Malaya during
prewar days but it was not until recently that the culti-
vetion of the plant was done on an extensive scale. By
Jananese hands there will be more than enough emetine
supply for the entire Co-Prosperity Sphere. Research
conducted by the Jaranese revealed that the drug was
obtainable even from one year old plants and also from
the leaves and stems of the plant. Several score tons
of the dried plant will soon be sent to Japan for manu-
facture of emetine which promises to be a boon to all
persons, especially those on the fighting front, suffering
from amoebic dysentery. (Domei in English).
131. Large Singapore Floating Dock Said Refloated.
Singanore, July 2l.- The world's largest floating
dock, souttled by the British at the Seletar Naval Base
before the fall of Singapore, has been refloated by Japan's
naval engineers after three months of skillful work,
demonstrating onee more the superiority of the imperial
navy's salvaging technique. The engineering skill of
the imperial navy in refloating the 50,000-ton dock
impressed several thousands of Chinese, Indian and Malay
workers in the employ of the navy, to say nothing of
Japanese residents here who witnessed the prodigious
task of salvaging the dock.
Constructed in England in 1928, the dook was
towed to Singapore in the following year to make the
Britimh naval outpost the"greatest naval base in the
Far Bast". With overall length of 855 feet and width of
172 feet and height of 75 feet, the dook itmelf weighs
20,000 tons and when empty displaees 120,000 tons. The
tromondous

=== Page 90 === (confidence 0.99)
-56-
tremendous buoyancy of the dock, that takes oare of a
capacity weight of a 50,000-ton vessel in addition to
1ts own weight of 20,000 tons, is created by 52 tanks
which occupy most of the wall space of the dock. Antici-
pating the inescapable fate of Singapore, the British
souttled the floating dock as early as the latter part
of December, 194l, by opening all 1ts valves and burning
off others at six places with ges torches. It was
towards the end of January this year that the imperial
navy first started making tests for refloating the dock
and two months were snent in actual investigation work.
In March, Japanese divers under the supervision of naval
engineers started repairs on the damaged parts. By the
early part of this month all repairs were completed and
powerful pumps especially installed to pump out the
tremendous amount of water were given their first tests.
With the test completed satisfactorily, work continued
until now the giant dock is again floating at the Seletar
naval station, adding much to the facilities of the port.
The feat is all the more remarkable when it is considered
that during the period the divers' work was interrupted...
(garbled)... (Domei in English).
132. Expansion of People's Bank in Malaya.
Sinsapore, July 21.- The People's Bank, which
was first established in Singapore on September 1 last
year and which has been playing an important role in
stabilizing the people's livelihood, has expanded with
branches throughout Malaya. The latest branch was
opened on July 15 in Kedah. Another will soon be estab-
lished at Selangor. The bank so far obtained loans amount-
ing to Yen 2,350,000 from the Southern Development Bank
of which Yen 1,080,000 has been lent out to 5,400 Malays,
Indians and Chinese inhabitants up to the end of May this
year. The loans are now given at an interest rate of
5 percent per year to all inhabitants including salaried
people, merohants and farmers who had been suffering from
exploitation of usurers. Consequently it is one of the
conspiouous results of this banking system that the number
of local usurors has been reduced to a fifth or a sixth of
the prewar level. Under these favorable results, interested
authorities, with a view of meeting the further need of
establishing the self-sufficient structure of Malayan
industry, are deliberating on a better aystem of making
loans. (Domei in English).

134. Rubber Used for Surfacing Airfield Runways.
Kuching, July 26.- Preliminary tests were
suceessfully made using crude rubber for surface coating
of

=== Page 91 === (confidence 0.98)
-57-
of airfield runways here. As a substitute for asphalt
or cement, crude rubber is excellent in the construction
of runways since it has the qualities of simplicity,
ofriciency of application and durability. Especially
in the southern regions, crude rubber runways are
practical because common cement or asphalt is likely to
be spoiled by heavy rains characteristio of the tropics.
Tests have proved that crude rubber runways undergo no
changes under the peculiar weather conditions in the
southern regions. The process of laying rubber runways
moreover is very simple. Crude rubber is merely coated
on prepared ground. Repairing of rubber runways likewise
is a simple process. Molten crude rubber is simply poured
over spots needing repair. Another merit is its dura-
bility. One defoct, however, of the crude rubber is its
odor but that soon will be eliminated by combining charcoal
powder with crude rubber. When the present tests are com-
pleted, the over-production of rubber in north Borneo will
be relieved most offectively through the newly discovered
use of rubber, thereby adding much to the stabilization of
the livelihood of the inhabitants who are mostly engaged in
rubber cultivation. (Domei in English).

consumption, the military administration here has decided

obtainable at low prices hereafter. (Domei in English).

141. Unified Code of Civil Laws for latives in
Indochina.
Hanoi, July 8.- Enactment of a unified code of
civil laws affecting the native inhabitents of French
Indochina is expected to be decided upon formally at a
gubernatorial conference scheduled to convene on July 20
at Dalet. It can be recalled that, at last year's confer-
ence, an agreement was reached to enact a unified code
applicable in all areas and draft regulations were drawn
up recently in conformity with this decision. The adontion
of the unified code and civil laws marks a clear cut
departure from the policy hitherto followed by the French
Indochina Government General of remaining strictly aloof
from disputes among the native inhabitants and of leaving
the solution of their legal disputes to established habits
and customs in the respective areas. The unified code
is expected to prevent complications which invariably arose
in the past due to the lack of unity in rules and regu-
lations. (Domei in English).

agreement to be enforoed retroactively. The quota of
Japanese importers will also be made effective retroactively
from January 1, under the new agreement. (Domei in English).

Saigon, July 16.- Complete agreement was reached
between Janan and French Indochina in a meeting yesterday
between the Jananese Consul General, Rokuro Suzuki, and
Jacques Martin, director of the economic bureau of the
govornment, with regard to the goods whioh will be
supplied to French Indochina by Japan this year. Formal
documents will be drafted shortly and are expected to
be approved and signed by representatives of the two
countries in Dalat. (Domei in nglish).
Saigon, July 25.- Regarding the agreement for
supplies from Indochina for the present fiscal year on
the basis of the economic agreement between Banan and
Indochina, the clas ifications and quantities of the
entire supply of material resources from Tndochina were
decided upon on l'arch 4. As to supplies of material
resources from Japan to Indochina, negotiations are
progressing rapidly. As a result of the conversation
between Consul Genoral Suzuki and '. Martin, chief of the
economic bureau, Indochina accented the Jananese proposal
as submitted. It is expected that the agreement will be
signed next week. (Domei in Jananese).
143. Commodity Interchange Cornoration of Janan.
Hanoi, July 14.- The Hano1 Foreign Trade Control
Association, which hitherto wes instrumental in effecting
a liaison between the Japanese ambassadorial office and
Japaneee trading firms, has been complet ly absorbed by
the Commodity Interchange Corporation of Janan. The
association, which wes formed by Jaranese firms in north
Indochina with Hanoi and Haiphong as the centers of acti-
vities, played an important role in fixing quotas and the
smooth distribution of imports and exports between French
Indochina and Japan. (Domei in English).
144. Subsidies for Speedy Delivery of Unhulled Rice.
Saigon, July 16.- The French Indochina Government
General promulgated new regulations providing subsidies
to native farmers for speedy delivery of unhulled rice
during the present harvest season. The regutations also
carry a new list of prices for various grades of unhulled
rice whereunder the price of rice produced in the hinterland
districts was raised to four piastres from three. The
regulations also state that farmers delivering unhulled
rice from the hinterland during July will be paid 4.35
niastres per picul, including a subsidy of 35 cents.
(Domei in English).
145. To Discuss Political and Economic Condition
of Indochina.
Saigon, July 19.- A conference of administrative
leaders of Frenoh Indochina is scheduled to be held for
three days from July 21 at Dalat under the chairmanship
of Governor Ceneral Jean Decoux to discuss the political
and economic condition of Freneh Indochina as well as the
consolidation of the country. Attending the conference
will be the Freneh resident superiors of Cambodia, Annam,
Tonkin and Laos, representatives of the Government, three
General. (Domei in English).
